Cancer dependency research with an autonomous RSI loop

Goal and executable handoff for a local research agent

Prepared 28 October 2026. Treat every source below as a seed to verify against the current primary source, release manifest, and paper methods.


1 - The ambitious goal

Build and run a laptop-based autonomous research system that answers a specific, still-open question about when molecularly defined cancer dependencies persist or fail across measured culture environments.

The primary question is whether measured sensitivity to culture conditions, together with genotype and tumor state, can explain failures of dependency transfer from conventional cancer models to patient-derived organoids beyond lineage and technical variation alone.

Produce all of the following.

- At least one previously unreported, precisely bounded, externally supported explanatory finding. It may be a positive conditional dependency rule, a convincing refutation of a meaningful dependency claim, or an informative bound that materially changes how a target should be interpreted. It must resolve a specific uncertainty, not merely increase a benchmark score.
- A reproducible evidence atlas covering every eligible gene in the controlled environmental panel, including support, contradiction, heterogeneity, uncertainty, missing evidence, and excluded genes with reasons. Expansion beyond that selected panel is exploratory unless independently justified.
- A defensible connection between measured environmental sensitivity and transfer behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort, where the available evidence supports that connection.
- An implemented research-policy improvement loop with real experiments, faithful historical replay, executable policy revisions, fresh deployment, and a final comparison against strong fixed exploration strategies.
- Evidence that the learned policy produces a practically meaningful improvement under matched total resources, including its own learning overhead.
- A manuscript and complete reproducibility package ready for independent expert review, with all findings, failures, limitations, and costs disclosed.

Seek a finding that changes which cancer target evidence researchers should trust. Do not substitute a generic prediction leaderboard or an agent framework demonstration for that scientific objective.

Ambition does not authorize changing the standard of evidence. If the scientific result or the policy improvement fails its criteria, the overall goal remains unmet. A valid negative result can qualify only when it resolves a predeclared meaningful uncertainty with sufficient precision. An insignificant p-value does not qualify.


2 - Your operating role

You are the lead investigator, research engineer, statistician, and experiment coordinator for this project.

Execute the work. Do not stop after writing a plan, creating folders, or describing an architecture. Use the current session to establish the scientific contract, build the smallest working vertical slice, evaluate real public observations, and begin the first actual policy-improvement cycle when the evidence and resources permit.

Use frontier model reasoning through the capabilities already supplied by your host agent. All scientific fitting, simulation, evaluation, and data processing must run on this laptop. Public web access and public data services are allowed. No new wet-lab measurements, controlled-access patient data, paid scientific cloud compute, or local foundation-model training are required.

Make routine research and implementation decisions autonomously. Ask only when a missing credential, explicit spending authority, unavailable required file, or genuinely consequential unresolved user choice prevents further authorized work. Complete all independent work first.

Work inside an isolated project directory named cancer-dependency-rsi under the current workspace, or continue the existing project if its identity is unambiguous. Preserve unrelated files and existing instructions.

Install dependencies in a project environment. Respect existing permissions and approval controls. Do not copy permission-bypass flags from upstream agent launch scripts. Do not submit papers, publish repositories, contact researchers, purchase services, or provision new infrastructure.

Use specialist workers when available, with distinct roles for literature and novelty, data provenance, scientific modeling, evaluator implementation, and adversarial review. The evaluator and final data access must have a narrower interface than the proposing agent. Shared privileged access is not a genuine security boundary and must never be described as one.


3 - Mandatory starting evidence

Verify and read the methods, data availability, supplements, and closest relevant findings before treating any seed as an established fact.

S1 - Broad NextGen resource and environmental experiments

https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13581591/
https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-026-10843-7
https://github.com/broadinstitute/DepMap-NextGen-Public
https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.29472362

S2 - Sanger organoid resource

https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13581617/
https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-026-10830-y
https://github.com/Garnett-Lab/SangerOrganoidBiobank
https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.28339340
https://cellmodelpassports.sanger.ac.uk/downloads
https://dataminer.depmap.sanger.ac.uk/
https://depmap.sanger.ac.uk/documentation/datasets/wg-crispr-knockout/

S3 - Closest direct transferability prior

https://doi.org/10.3390/biology15191782
https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42883455/
https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13664187/
https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22472622

S4 - Current Broad data and release guidance

https://depmap.org/portal/data_page/?tab=allData
https://forum.depmap.org/t/organoid-dependency-data/4671
https://forum.depmap.org/t/announcing-the-26q3-release/4692

S5 - Dream-RSI method and implementation

https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14858
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.14858v2
https://github.com/google-research/dream-rsi

The starting literature establishes several constraints.

- The Broad resource reports 147 genome-scale screens, while the Sanger resource reports screens across 162 organoids. These counts are not automatically disjoint biological samples.
- The smaller controlled Broad environmental experiment has 250 deliberately selected test genes and 18 screens across only seven biological models. It includes seven matched format contrasts and five matched medium contrasts, with possible repeated models. Verify the exact design in the source.
- The 250 genes were selected partly because they differed between model environments. They are not a representative genome-wide sample.
- The October 2026 transferability study already examines expression-based transfer from conventional models to Broad and Sanger organoids for 20 selected genes. Generic 2D-to-organoid transfer is not the new contribution.
- Broad's temporary NextGen manuscript dataset was incorporated into DepMap Public 26Q3. Staff direct users to the integrated CRISPRGeneEffect.csv and relevant model metadata. Reverify the current route rather than inventing a file ID or assuming old instructions still work.
- Sanger processed fitness measurements and Broad Chronos gene effects are different measurement systems. Their absolute values are not interchangeable.
- Historical releases, a current integrated release, and independently produced laboratory data are different forms of evidence. Reprocessing the same observations is not biological replication.

Resolve downloads through current official manifests. Record source URL, release or commit, retrieval date, license, byte size, and checksum. Do not invent direct download URLs or accession metadata. Use official alternative routes if a public portal blocks unattended retrieval. Do not bypass a challenge or access control.

Build around public processed observations. Raw sequencing is unnecessary. If a proposed analysis requires paired observations that are not actually released, identify that limitation before implementing the model.


4 - Hardware, budget, and persistence

Inspect the available RAM, CPU, disk, operating system, installed runtimes, and already configured model access without printing secrets.

Set conservative defaults and save them in a machine-readable configuration.

- No GPU dependency.
- At most two CPU-heavy jobs concurrently.
- An aggregate research memory ceiling of the smaller of 8 GiB and 60 percent of physical RAM, reduced further when necessary to leave the host responsive.
- A ten-minute default timeout for an individual exploratory fitting job. Longer jobs require an explicit recorded allocation from the existing campaign budget.
- A download ceiling of 20 GiB or one quarter of available disk space, whichever is smaller.
- A first pilot target of 24 real evaluated development nodes and three executable policy challengers, retaining the incumbent.
- An eight-hour ceiling for the first pilot invocation. This is a safety ceiling, not a promised completion time.
- No automatic increase in financial, disk, concurrency, or wall-time ceilings.

Use the host agent's already authorized model access. Any additional programmatic metered API calls require a numeric user-configured cap or an existing explicitly authorized cap. If none exists, the additional API spending cap is zero. Continue with host-driven reasoning and local computation where possible, and build the adapter without dispatching unapproved calls.

Do not require the user to buy new services just to start the project. Do not silently equate an unknown price with zero.

For separately dispatched API calls, enforce limits in code. Reserve a conservative maximum cost before each request, count concurrent reservations and retries, reconcile against returned usage, and persist the ledger before dispatch. Do not call a provider whose billable maximum cannot be bounded by the configured limits.

Record model identifiers, prompts, token counts when exposed, tool costs, wall time, CPU time, and peak memory. If host-model costs cannot be measured, mark them unknown. You may still do science, but you cannot certify an all-in cost improvement while material costs are unobserved.

Use append-only event records and checkpoint after every completed experiment. Track proposed, dispatched, completed, failed, and committed states. An interrupted request remains reserved until reconciled. Never reset the budget or repeat a completed paid request on resume.

Do not claim a process is running unless you have an actual live process or job identifier and recent heartbeat. If the host session ends, write the exact next action and resume command. Do not promise work will continue after the runtime stops.


5 - Data and identifiability audit

Before adaptive hypothesis search, construct a provenance table with biological model, donor, tumor lineage, sample origin, laboratory, assay library, medium, growth format, replicate identity, release, and measured outcome.

Create explicit crosswalks among Broad, HCMI, Sanger, and author-specific identifiers. Mark uncertain donor identity as uncertain rather than assuming independence.

Determine which observations are truly paired. Count independent biological models and donors. Do not count genes, guide RNAs, wells, alternative cultures, or technical replicates as additional independent patients.

Audit release composition and preprocessing provenance. An integrated release may contain validation observations or corrections learned jointly from them. Removing overlapping rows alone does not undo leakage through outcome-guided target selection, normalization, or model selection. Prefer compatible study-specific releases and transformations fitted on development data. If a fitted correction used external dependency outcomes, rebuild a development-only analysis or obtain genuinely independent validation evidence. Disclosure alone does not restore independence. Distinguish that outcome leakage from covariate-only joint preprocessing, which must be disclosed as transductive feature preprocessing with appropriately limited claims. This is a required provenance check, not an assertion that any named release is contaminated.

Verify dependency score direction, dynamic range, controls, missingness, and quality flags separately for each source. Avoid direct subtraction of Chronos values from Sanger fold-change or essentiality scores.

Preserve the seven-model environmental design as a small, selected experiment. Determine whether row-level paired outcomes, replicates, and required metadata are sufficient for the intended contrast. A batch correction cannot separate laboratory and biology when the experimental design confounds them.

Record attrition explicitly, including models unable to grow in a condition and genes excluded for insufficient variation or precision.

Data eligibility is determined from prespecified quality and identifiability rules. It must not be selected later to maximize a favorable result.

The gate is an identifiable, falsifiable question in a named subset. If the data cannot support the original question, propose and document a narrower still-open question within the same scientific objective. Do not pretend the original broader goal has been achieved.


6 - Scientific estimands and research contract

Start from two connected estimands.

The direct environmental estimand is the change in a gene's measured dependency between environment A and environment B within the same biological model, while holding the other measured culture condition fixed. Analyze medium and growth format separately where the design permits.

The transfer estimand is the extent to which a prespecified genotype, tumor-state, or context relationship predicts dependency behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort beyond lineage and the strongest appropriate baseline.

Use original documented assay units for paired within-study contrasts. Use justified ranking, calibration, or outcome-specific comparisons for cross-study evidence.

For each environmental claim, report the dependency in both conditions, all individual paired changes, uncertainty, and heterogeneity. A near-zero average can conceal large opposing effects.

Do not call a dependency invariant because a difference is nonsignificant. If claiming equivalence, define a scientifically justified equivalence margin before confirmatory evaluation and require the relevant interval to fall inside that margin. If no defensible margin exists, report continuous effects and uncertainty without an invariance label.

A stable absence of dependency is not a stable cancer vulnerability. Establish that the dependency itself is meaningful in the environments covered by a positive claim.

Do not interpret a predictive expression feature as a causal mediator without identifying assumptions and suitable evidence. Environmental intervention evidence and cross-cohort biomarker associations must retain distinct evidence labels.

Carry uncertainty and shared-model dependence in estimated environmental sensitivity into downstream transfer analyses using a justified measurement-error, resampling, or sensitivity analysis. Do not treat noisy estimated sensitivities as exact independent inputs. Report conclusions that change materially across plausible environmental-effect estimates.

During initial development, choose and justify the precise primary endpoint, practical effect margin, hypothesis family, baseline set, uncertainty procedure, and sensitivity analyses. Then freeze them before policy optimization.

The research contract must include the following.

- Exact question and falsifiable alternatives.
- Intended population, environments, targets, and generalization claim.
- Eligibility and exclusion rules.
- Data and identifier versions.
- Development, policy-evaluation, and final external partitions.
- Feature engineering and transformation rules.
- Primary and secondary endpoints with fixed direction.
- Minimum practical effects or equivalence margins and their justification.
- Multiplicity control and uncertainty methods.
- Baseline definitions.
- Invalid-result rules.
- Success, failure, inconclusive, and budget-exhaustion conditions.

The initial lock fixes the permitted hypothesis and transformation spaces, partitions, evaluator, baseline definitions, practical margins, and inferential rules. Adaptive development may select hypotheses, features, hyperparameters, and fitted artifacts within those spaces. A second lock commits the exact selected claim family, predictions, and artifacts before external evaluation.

Hash this contract, evaluator code, and split manifests. Changing a locked scientific contract creates a new study identifier and requires appropriate new evaluation evidence.


7 - Novelty and biological scope

Maintain a claim ledger throughout the project.

For each proposed claim, record the nearest prior result, which part is already established, the precise remaining uncertainty, evidence needed to resolve it, and the consequence if the claim is supported or refuted.

Read the Broad environmental analyses, the Sanger biomarker analyses, and the October transfer study closely. Their reported lipid, adhesion, state, and dependency findings are controls or prior evidence. Recovering them is not a new discovery.

Use current primary literature, author data, and official documentation. Date the search and preserve queries and important exclusions. Absence of an exact search hit is not proof that a claim is new.

Choose a bounded hypothesis family before final evaluation. Consider gene classes with measured environmental sensitivity and adequate overlap, not an unconstrained scan of every feature until a small p-value appears.

Before finalizing the manuscript, repeat the novelty audit for each surviving exact claim. Preserve contradictions and prior evidence. Do not silently rename a known association to make it appear new.

The required scientific contribution must be more specific than weak organoid transfer, better average correlation, or successful agent automation.


8 - Baselines, inference, and adversarial checks

Build simple competent baselines before allowing elaborate models.

Before the final lock, reproduce baselines only on development data or independent control data. Any reproduction requiring final external outcomes must occur after the final hypotheses, predictions, artifacts, and inferential code are committed, alongside the final evaluation. Those results cannot inform revisions to the tested study.

Where applicable compare lineage only, genotype only, tumor-state or expression only, genotype plus state, measured context or assay covariates, and a regularized linear predictor modeled on the existing transfer study.

Use the same eligible observations and permitted information for each comparison. Learn preprocessing, feature selection, dimension reduction, and tuning within development folds. Do not optimize a feature representation on final external outcomes.

Compare against the strongest applicable baseline, not only the weakest. If environmental information is central to the explanation, include a prespecified ablation removing it.

Use effect sizes and calibrated predictive or uncertainty endpoints as the scientific evaluator's optimization target. Do not reward p-value reduction, the number of discoveries, manuscript length, or confidence of the LLM explanation.

Treat adaptive development as exploratory. Evaluate a locked final claim family once on independent outcomes. For a small confirmatory family, Holm correction at familywise alpha 0.05 is a reasonable default. Any alternative must be justified and frozen before outcomes are revealed.

Do not apply ordinary fixed-family correction to an indefinitely adaptive stream of repeatedly inspected p-values and call it confirmatory evidence.

Use uncertainty at the donor or biological-model level, accounting for repeated cultures and target or pathway dependence where relevant. Many bootstrap repetitions do not create new biological replication. With few clusters, assess whether the proposed interval or test is justified and report the effective sample size.

Perform relevant negative controls, leakage checks, donor and lineage sensitivity analyses, platform sensitivity, and leave-one-model-out checks. Permutations must respect exchangeability and grouping. Do not permute labels freely when the design makes that invalid.

Synthetic data may test code, calibration, and identifiability under explicit assumptions. Synthetic labels, imputed outcomes, and LLM guesses cannot serve as independent biological validation.

Only make claims supported by the actual evidence. Public dependency screens alone do not establish clinical efficacy, normal-tissue safety, treatment resistance reversal, or universal target validity.


9 - Three distinct evaluation layers

Keep these layers separate.

A - Development task panels

Use them to build scientific artifacts, tune development models, generate discovery trees, and select policy challengers. Record every adaptive use.

B - Reserved policy evaluation panels

Use them to compare the final learned exploration policy with fixed strategies in fresh rollouts. Freeze the compared policies and selection criterion before these panels are evaluated.

C - Final external biological evaluation

Prefer an independently filtered Sanger cohort after resolving donor overlap, release provenance, assay compatibility, and adequate representation. Use final outcomes only after committing the hypotheses, predictions, model artifacts, and inferential code.

A final external cohort cannot also be a repeatedly consulted policy reward. Do not send final Sanger outcomes into the replay pool.

For each final claim, identify exactly which component each dataset validates. External confirmation of dependency transfer does not independently validate a medium or format effect, or establish that environmental sensitivity caused transfer failure. Retain adaptively reused environmental evidence as exploratory or selection-adjusted evidence, as justified. Do not manufacture an independent environmental test by splitting genes from the same seven models. Seek an additional existing public controlled-environment dataset when the selected claim requires independent environmental confirmation. If none is suitable, narrow the claim or retain the unmet evidence gate.

Subdividing the selected 250 genes does not create hundreds of independent biological tasks. Record model, donor, gene, and pathway overlap in all panels, and use blocked evaluation. The larger development cohorts may provide engineering tasks for policy learning, but their transfer to the small environmental problem must be reported honestly.

Within a single privileged local agent, enforce a logged protocol with a separate evaluator interface and restricted inputs. Do not claim cryptographic secrecy or genuine blindness merely because an outcome file lives in a separate directory.

Use retrospective external validation as the default wording. Public literature and model pretraining can expose outcomes. Record known exposure and do not certify that public results were unknowable.

A failed final test is a failed final test. Subsequent work that uses its outcomes becomes exploratory unless genuinely new independent evidence is obtained.


10 - Implement the actual RSI loop

Keep the base scientific generator, its model version and prompting contract, data contract, and evaluator fixed during a policy comparison.

Make the editable policy an executable module that receives observed history, legal actions, and remaining resources, and selects an action.

Legal actions may include opening a predefined analysis family, choosing an existing branch, refining a particular artifact, running a permitted diagnostic, and stopping.

The policy may alter branching, prioritization, exploration versus refinement, and stopping. It cannot change labels, splits, metrics, practical margins, multiplicity correction, or success criteria.

A narrative reflection is not a policy improvement unless it changes executed decisions.

Record each discovery node's immutable parent, exact artifact, input context hash, data and split hashes, evaluator hash, seeds, result, validity diagnostics, failures, model calls, and measured costs.

Historical replay may reveal only previously executed artifacts and their actual recorded outcomes. It must not use a model to invent outcomes for unexplored analyses.

A reordered branch may alter the context seen by a generator. Make refinement depend on an immutable parent and fixed context, or require an exact context match before replaying a recorded child. Do not assume a recorded child represents an arbitrary counterfactual generation.

The replay policy sees only the outcomes revealed along its trajectory. Do not expose the full tree's future rewards.

Mark unsupported replay actions unsupported. Do not assign them fake scores or treat a surrogate prediction as a measured outcome. Compare policies on common support, report support coverage, and use replay as a screening tool.

Perform the following cycle.

1. Run the initial fixed policy on real development tasks.
2. Preserve the evaluated discovery trees as replay worlds.
3. Generate at least three executable policy challengers plus the unchanged incumbent.
4. Replay them under the same resource constraints and support rules.
5. Select a challenger with the frozen selection rule.
6. Test the challenger in fresh development rollouts against fixed exploration using the same generator and evaluator.
7. Promote only when the preregistered criterion is met.
8. Retain the incumbent when challengers fail.
9. Add only development histories and repeat.

Complete at least two genuine cycles before the final policy comparison if the authorized resources and valid tasks permit. Two cycles do not require two promotions. An unchanged incumbent is a valid experimental outcome.

After development, freeze the best admissible policy and evaluate it on the reserved policy panels. Compare it with the original fixed exploration strategy and at least one simple fixed-allocation or random-selection strategy.


11 - What counts as demonstrated policy improvement

Predeclare one primary operating point or cost-to-quality endpoint. Also report complete scientific-quality-versus-cost curves.

Use the same generator, evaluator, initial artifacts, information permissions, and common resource ceilings for all policies.

Charge policy rewriting, replay, failed jobs, promotion trials, and extra model calls to the improving system. Permit fixed baselines to spend the corresponding budget on scientific search.

Report both end-to-end performance including learning overhead and deployment-only performance with learning costs shown separately.

The ambitious engineering target is at least 20 percent lower total measured API expenditure to reach a predeclared valid scientific-quality threshold, under the same CPU ceiling and information constraints, compared with the strongest fixed baseline. This is a project target, not a forecast.

Set the quality threshold from development evidence before final policy evaluation. It must be meaningful and attainable by competent baselines, not chosen after observing which policy wins.

Use paired blocked comparisons where appropriate and report uncertainty. For the default 20 percent savings goal, require the prespecified one-sided 95 percent lower confidence bound on savings to meet the 20 percent practical margin. A point estimate above 20 percent with a lower bound merely above zero supports some improvement but does not meet that ambitious margin. Freeze how failures to reach the quality threshold within budget are treated as censored outcomes. Do not convert nonattainment into invented exact costs. Random seeds quantify execution variability, not independent biological replication.

If API expenditure is not the dominant or measurable resource, choose a justified alternative primary resource endpoint during the initial contract stage, explain it, and lock it before optimization. All other material resources must still be measured or strictly matched. A CPU-only advantage or token proxy cannot satisfy C9 while material host-model costs remain unknown and unmatched. In that case retain an unresolved cost gate and avoid claiming all-in savings. Define an equally explicit practical improvement margin and uncertainty requirement for any alternative endpoint. Do not switch endpoints after seeing final results.

If faithful replay has inadequate support, preserve that finding and use fresh rollouts to assess the proposed policy. Label the method accurately as a Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation and disclose departures.

Do not call the system recursively self-improving merely because more compute improved a reused validation score.


12 - Completion criteria

Mark the ambitious goal complete only when every applicable criterion below has documentary evidence.

C1 - A current novelty audit identifies a precise unresolved cancer dependency question and distinguishes it from the August resources and October transfer study.

C2 - All analyses use legitimately accessible public data, with complete provenance, licensing, identifier crosswalks, and reproducible retrieval or documented local cache installation.

C3 - The intended paired contrast or conditional relationship is identifiable within a named population. Confounding and selection are disclosed, and independent biological units are counted correctly.

C4 - The scientific contract, eligibility rules, splits, baseline set, practical effects, evaluator, and confirmatory procedures were frozen before adaptive optimization and final evaluation.

C5 - The pipeline reproduces relevant published or author-code baseline results closely enough to establish outcome direction, cohort integrity, and measurement interpretation, with discrepancies resolved or explicitly bounded.

C6 - At least one previously unreported explanatory finding passes the frozen scientific criteria and relevant external tests. A supported refutation or informative bound may qualify when it resolves a meaningful predeclared uncertainty. Mere nonsignificance, a rediscovered association, or a better exploratory score does not qualify.

C7 - The evidence atlas covers all eligible genes in the controlled panel and lists every exclusion. Every entry distinguishes dependency strength, environmental change, uncertainty, heterogeneity, biomarker evidence, transfer evidence, and prior knowledge.

C8 - At least two real policy-improvement cycles have run, with executable challengers, faithful replay, fresh rollout evaluation, retained failed attempts, and a final frozen-policy comparison on reserved panels.

C9 - The learned policy meets the preregistered practical improvement criterion against the strongest fixed baseline under matched total resources. Meeting only a deployment-only criterion does not satisfy an end-to-end claim.

C10 - Scientific conclusions survive the prespecified multiplicity correction, appropriate uncertainty analysis, key ablations, and adversarial checks. Any claim of invariance requires positive dependency evidence and justified equivalence evidence.

C11 - A clean reproduction run regenerates the primary estimates, figures, evidence decisions, and policy comparison within documented tolerances from frozen artifacts. It must not require rerunning stochastic LLM search to reproduce the reported analysis.

C12 - The package includes a manuscript for expert review, machine-readable evidence, source and data manifests, all experiment histories, model and policy versions, actual resource measurements, failures, limitations, and exact reproduce and resume commands.

C13 - The first meaningful end-to-end research cycle has actually executed. A plan, scaffold, mock evaluator, or hypothetical example is insufficient.

Seek multiple convergent findings across adequately represented lineages and pathways as an additional scientific stretch target. Do not replace one strong finding with a quota of weak associations. Do not force claims about sparsely represented cancer types.

Keep scientific discovery, prediction improvement, and policy improvement as separate status fields. One does not establish the others.


13 - Required artifacts and commands

Create a compact repository with equivalent artifacts to the following. Choose actual file formats suited to the content.

- README.md with scope, installation, exact commands, and current limitations.
- GOAL.md with the locked goal and completion criteria.
- PROTOCOL.md and a machine-readable locked research contract.
- STATUS.md and a structured status record with current phase, incumbent, spending, next action, and unmet gates.
- A data manifest, source manifest, donor/model crosswalk, eligibility registry, and split manifests.
- A novelty and claim ledger with the complete decision history.
- Data adapters, baseline models, a fixed evaluator, policy modules, a faithful replay engine, a local runner, and a budget wrapper.
- An append-only experiment ledger and immutable discovery artifacts.
- Frozen final predictions, scientific models, policies, and checksums.
- An evidence atlas in a machine-readable table.
- Figures, a manuscript draft, a limitations statement, and an adversarial-review report.
- Environment and dependency locks, licenses and attribution, and a reproducibility report.
- Meaningful tests for identity leakage, score direction, grouping, estimator behavior, cost accounting, resume idempotence, unsupported replay actions, and final-outcome access.

Provide simple documented commands equivalent to bootstrap, audit-data, reproduce-baselines, run-pilot, run-cycle, evaluate-policy, lock-science, evaluate-external, reproduce, status, and resume.

Do not overbuild a general agent platform before the first valid scientific vertical slice works. Reuse maintained components where that reduces risk, but inspect their resource demands and permissions.


14 - Terminal outcomes and reporting

Use explicit terminal states.

- COMPLETE_SUPPORTED_DISCOVERY
- COMPLETE_SUPPORTED_REFUTATION_OR_BOUND
- SCIENTIFIC_RESULT_WITHOUT_RSI_EVIDENCE
- RSI_RESULT_WITHOUT_SCIENTIFIC_DISCOVERY
- INCONCLUSIVE
- BLOCKED
- BUDGET_EXHAUSTED

The first two require every relevant completion gate, including demonstrated policy improvement. The others are honest outcomes and must list which parts of the ambitious goal remain unmet.

A completed computational campaign is not automatically a completed scientific goal. Do not relabel insufficient power as equivalence or a polished manuscript as a discovery.

When blocked or budget-limited, checkpoint all valid work, identify the exact missing condition, and provide the smallest concrete next step. Do not lower scientific standards, spend beyond the cap, or retry indefinitely.

During execution, report concise milestones with measured results, unresolved risks, and the next discriminating experiment. Avoid progress theater based on file counts or unexecuted plans.


15 - Start now

Inspect the workspace and resource limits. Verify the primary sources and accessible processed data. Build the donor and assay provenance audit. Formulate a narrowly identifiable first question and its strongest alternative explanations.

Implement the smallest valid data-to-estimate-to-evaluator path. Reproduce the necessary baseline. Freeze the initial research contract and evaluation partitions.

Then run real development experiments, build the first replay world, generate executable policy challengers, and perform a fresh comparison. Continue through the authorized staged campaign.

Your first substantive report must show what was actually executed, what the observations establish, what remains unidentifiable, which policy was retained or promoted, the measured costs, and the exact next action. If a real evidence or authorization gate prevents the first cycle, report that precise blocker and all completed independent work. Do not claim that the loop started when only its scaffolding exists.

    85	The starting literature establishes several constraints.
    86	
    87	- The Broad resource reports 147 genome-scale screens, while the Sanger resource reports screens across 162 organoids. These counts are not automatically disjoint biological samples.
    88	- The smaller controlled Broad environmental experiment has 250 deliberately selected test genes and 18 screens across only seven biological models. It includes seven matched format contrasts and five matched medium contrasts, with possible repeated models. Verify the exact design in the source.
    89	- The 250 genes were selected partly because they differed between model environments. They are not a representative genome-wide sample.
    90	- The October 2026 transferability study already examines expression-based transfer from conventional models to Broad and Sanger organoids for 20 selected genes. Generic 2D-to-organoid transfer is not the new contribution.
    91	- Broad's temporary NextGen manuscript dataset was incorporated into DepMap Public 26Q3. Staff direct users to the integrated CRISPRGeneEffect.csv and relevant model metadata. Reverify the current route rather than inventing a file ID or assuming old instructions still work.
    92	- Sanger processed fitness measurements and Broad Chronos gene effects are different measurement systems. Their absolute values are not interchangeable.
    93	- Historical releases, a current integrated release, and independently produced laboratory data are different forms of evidence. Reprocessing the same observations is not biological replication.
    94	
    95	Resolve downloads through current official manifests. Record source URL, release or commit, retrieval date, license, byte size, and checksum. Do not invent direct download URLs or accession metadata. Use official alternative routes if a public portal blocks unattended retrieval. Do not bypass a challenge or access control.

   165	Do not interpret a predictive expression feature as a causal mediator without identifying assumptions and suitable evidence. Environmental intervention evidence and cross-cohort biomarker associations must retain distinct evidence labels.
   166	
   167	Carry uncertainty and shared-model dependence in estimated environmental sensitivity into downstream transfer analyses using a justified measurement-error, resampling, or sensitivity analysis. Do not treat noisy estimated sensitivities as exact independent inputs. Report conclusions that change materially across plausible environmental-effect estimates.
   168	
   169	During initial development, choose and justify the precise primary endpoint, practical effect margin, hypothesis family, baseline set, uncertainty procedure, and sensitivity analyses. Then freeze them before policy optimization.
   170	
   171	The research contract must include the following.
   172

   174	- Intended population, environments, targets, and generalization claim.
   175	- Eligibility and exclusion rules.
   176	- Data and identifier versions.
   177	- Development, policy-evaluation, and final external partitions.
   178	- Feature engineering and transformation rules.

   180	- Minimum practical effects or equivalence margins and their justification.

   182	- Baseline definitions.
   183	- Invalid-result rules.
   184	- Success, failure, inconclusive, and budget-exhaustion conditions.
   185	
   186	The initial lock fixes the permitted hypothesis and transformation spaces, partitions, evaluator, baseline definitions, practical margins, and inferential rules. Adaptive development may select hypotheses, features, hyperparameters, and fitted artifacts within those spaces. A second lock commits the exact selected claim family, predictions, and artifacts before external evaluation.
   187

   264	10 - Implement the actual RSI loop
   265	
   266	Keep the base scientific generator, its model version and prompting contract, data contract, and evaluator fixed during a policy comparison.
   267	
   268	Make the editable policy an executable module that receives observed history, legal actions, and remaining resources, and selects an action.
   269	
   270	Legal actions may include opening a predefined analysis family, choosing an existing branch, refining a particular artifact, running a permitted diagnostic, and stopping.
   271	
   272	The policy may alter branching, prioritization, exploration versus refinement, and stopping. It cannot change labels, splits, metrics, practical margins, multiplicity correction, or success criteria.
   273	
   274	A narrative reflection is not a policy improvement unless it changes executed decisions.
   275	
   276	Record each discovery node's immutable parent, exact artifact, input context hash, data and split hashes, evaluator hash, seeds, result, validity diagnostics, failures, model calls, and measured costs.
   277	
   278	Historical replay may reveal only previously executed artifacts and their actual recorded outcomes. It must not use a model to invent outcomes for unexplored analyses.
   279	
   280	A reordered branch may alter the context seen by a generator. Make refinement depend on an immutable parent and fixed context, or require an exact context match before replaying a recorded child. Do not assume a recorded child represents an arbitrary counterfactual generation.
   281	
   282	The replay policy sees only the outcomes revealed along its trajectory. Do not expose the full tree's future rewards.
   283	
   284	Mark unsupported replay actions unsupported. Do not assign them fake scores or treat a surrogate prediction as a measured outcome. Compare policies on common support, report support coverage, and use replay as a screening tool.
   285	
   286	Perform the following cycle.
   287	
   288	1. Run the initial fixed policy on real development tasks.
   289	2. Preserve the evaluated discovery trees as replay worlds.
   290	3. Generate at least three executable policy challengers plus the unchanged incumbent.
   291	4. Replay them under the same resource constraints and support rules.
   292	5. Select a challenger with the frozen selection rule.
   293	6. Test the challenger in fresh development rollouts against fixed exploration using the same generator and evaluator.
   294	7. Promote only when the preregistered criterion is met.
   295	8. Retain the incumbent when challengers fail.
   296	9. Add only development histories and repeat.
   297	
   298	Complete at least two genuine cycles before the final policy comparison if the authorized resources and valid tasks permit. Two cycles do not require two promotions. An unchanged incumbent is a valid experimental outcome.
   299	
   300	After development, freeze the best admissible policy and evaluate it on the reserved policy panels. Compare it with the original fixed exploration strategy and at least one simple fixed-allocation or random-selection strategy.
   301

   303	11 - What counts as demonstrated policy improvement
   304	
   305	Predeclare one primary operating point or cost-to-quality endpoint. Also report complete scientific-quality-versus-cost curves.
   306

   308	
   309	Charge policy rewriting, replay, failed jobs, promotion trials, and extra model calls to the improving system. Permit fixed baselines to spend the corresponding budget on scientific search.
   310	
   311	Report both end-to-end performance including learning overhead and deployment-only performance with learning costs shown separately.
   312	
   313	The ambitious engineering target is at least 20 percent lower total measured API expenditure to reach a predeclared valid scientific-quality threshold, under the same CPU ceiling and information constraints, compared with the strongest fixed baseline. This is a project target, not a forecast.
   314	
   315	Set the quality threshold from development evidence before final policy evaluation. It must be meaningful and attainable by competent baselines, not chosen after observing which policy wins.
   316	
   317	Use paired blocked comparisons where appropriate and report uncertainty. For the default 20 percent savings goal, require the prespecified one-sided 95 percent lower confidence bound on savings to meet the 20 percent practical margin. A point estimate above 20 percent with a lower bound merely above zero supports some improvement but does not meet that ambitious margin. Freeze how failures to reach the quality threshold within budget are treated as censored outcomes. Do not convert nonattainment into invented exact costs. Random seeds quantify execution variability, not independent biological replication.
   318	
   319	If API expenditure is not the dominant or measurable resource, choose a justified alternative primary resource endpoint during the initial contract stage, explain it, and lock it before optimization. In that case retain an unresolved cost gate and avoid claiming all-in savings. Define an equally explicit practical improvement margin and uncertainty requirement for any alternative endpoint. Do not switch endpoints after seeing final results.

   322	
   323	Do not call the system recursively self-improving merely because more compute improved a reused validation score.

     1	# PLAN — Executing the Cancer-Dependency RSI Research Loop
     2	
     3	**Specification:** `cancer_rsi_handoff.txt` (28 Oct 2026) · **Locked goal:** `GOAL.md` · **Project root:** `cancer-dependency-rsi/`
     4	**Host (verified 2026-10-28):** Apple M5 Max, 18 CPU cores, 128 GB RAM, 532 GiB free disk, macOS 26.6 (arm64); Python 3.14.8, git 2.54, Node available; no R/Julia (install only if a baseline requires it, inside the project env).
     5	**Model access (verified):** Muse configured with `gpt-6.1-sol` (`~/.codex/config.toml`: `model_reasoning_effort="high"`); host agent available for reasoning. All 8 seed URLs (S1–S5 papers/repos/portals) returned HTTP 200 on 2026-10-28 — reachability only; content/methods verification is Phase 1 work, not yet done.
     6	
     7	This plan is the execution contract for the loop. It sequences the work so that **no adaptive search happens before identifiability is proven, no policy optimization happens before the evaluator is frozen, and no final outcome is touched before the second lock.** Each phase ends at an evidence gate; failing a gate narrows the question or stops honestly — it never lowers the standard.
     8	
     9	---
    10	
    11	## 0. Operating rules (bind every phase)
    12	
    13	### 0.1 Model routing (user-mandated, enforced in code)
    14	| Role | Model | Reasoning | Invocation pattern |
    15	|---|---|---|---|
    16	| Adversarial review of **everything** (plans, code, contracts, claims, manuscript) | `gpt-6.1-sol` | `high` | `codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort='"high"' -s read-only "<review prompt>"` |
    17	| Generating new generations (policy challengers, hypothesis/analysis candidates) | `gpt-6.1-sol` | `xhigh` | `codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort='"xhigh"' -s workspace-write "<generation prompt>"` |
    18	| Lead-investigator reasoning, statistics, implementation | Host agent | — | Local session; all fitting/evaluation runs locally in Python |
    19	
    20	- The **review protocol** is fixed: reviewer must enumerate issues by severity (**low / medium / high / critical**) with file:line evidence and a single **0–100 score**. The lead investigator **verifies each issue against the artifact** (accept / reject with reason), fixes all verified issues, and **re-reviews until score ≥ 90 and zero verified high/critical issues remain**. Review transcripts, verdicts, and fixes are appended to `reviews/` and the experiment ledger. A score is never accepted without its issue list; an issue is never fixed without verification.
    21	- Specialist worker roles are kept distinct: literature/novelty, data provenance, scientific modeling, evaluator implementation, adversarial review. The evaluator process receives a **narrower interface** than the proposer (hashed inputs, no final-outcome paths). This is a logged protocol, **not** a cryptographic security boundary — never described as one.
    22	- Every model call is recorded: model ID, effort, prompt hash, token counts when exposed, wall/CPU seconds, peak RSS, monetary cost or `unknown`. **Additional metered API spend cap = $0** unless the user configures a numeric cap; host/codex access already authorized is used, with unmeasurable cost marked `unknown` (which blocks certifying all-in C9 savings — see §7.3).
    23	
    24	### 0.2 Resource envelope (written to `cancer-dependency-rsi/config/resources.json`, enforced by the budget wrapper)
    25	- No GPU. ≤ 2 CPU-heavy jobs concurrently. Aggregate research RSS ceiling = min(8 GiB, 60% RAM) = **8 GiB** on this host.
    26	- Exploratory fit timeout **600 s** default; longer only via explicit recorded allocation from the campaign budget.
    27	- Downloads ≤ min(20 GiB, ¼ free disk) = **20 GiB**. First pilot: **24 real evaluated development nodes + 3 executable challengers + incumbent**, ≤ **8 h** wall (safety ceiling, not a promise).
    28	- No automatic ceiling increases. Budget wrapper **reserves before dispatch, persists before dispatch, reconciles after**; interrupted ⇒ stays `reserved` until reconciled; completed paid/host calls are never repeated on resume.
    29	
    30	### 0.3 Persistence & honesty primitives
    31	- Append-only JSONL ledgers: `ledger/experiments.jsonl`, `ledger/resources.jsonl`, `ledger/reviews.jsonl`. Node states: `proposed → dispatched → completed|failed → committed`. Checkpoint after **every** completed experiment.
    32	- Every discovery node stores: immutable parent ID, artifact bytes + SHA-256, input-context hash, data hash, split hash, evaluator hash, generator model+effort+prompt hash, seeds, result, validity diagnostics, failures, measured costs.
    33	- `STATUS.md` + `status.json` always carry: phase, incumbent policy ID, spend, three separate status fields (`scientific_discovery`, `prediction_improvement`, `policy_improvement`), unmet gates (C1–C13), **next action**, and **exact resume command**. Never claim the loop is "running" without a live PID/job ID + recent heartbeat.
    34	- Terminal states only from the GOAL list. A failed final test stays failed; downstream work on its outcomes is exploratory.
    35	
    36	### 0.4 The trajectory system — how tried directions are tracked, scored, kept, recombined (Dream-RSI adaptation)
    37	
    38	This is the engine of the loop, adapted from Google's Dream-RSI (arXiv:2609.14858; `google-research/dream-rsi`): accumulated discovery history acts as a **replay simulator** over the realized search space; policies are improved **offline by "dreaming" in replay worlds** and only then redeployed online; base generator, evaluator, and task stay fixed while only the exploration policy changes. Departures required by the cancer specification (faithful-replay-only, fixed evaluator/splits/margins, no invented outcomes) are binding and disclosed — where they force a change from the paper, the method is labeled a **Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation**.
    39	
    40	**0.4.1 Units of record (nothing tried is ever lost or rewritten).**
    41	- **Node (attempt):** one evaluated artifact — the atomic unit. Fields per §0.3 plus: `direction_id`, `branch_id`, `generation`, `origin` (see 0.4.4), evaluator score vector (quality endpoints, validity, cost), `outcome_class` ∈ {`success`, `conceptual_failure`, `implementation_failure`, `invalid_result`, `unsupported`, `unknown`}, and free-text `lesson` (what this attempt ruled in/out).
    42	- **Branch / trajectory:** an ordered root-to-leaf chain of refinements: root artifact + up to `REFINE` children, mirroring Dream-RSI's `BRANCHES × (REFINE+1)` tree shape (paper default 10×11; here scaled to the pilot budget, e.g. pilot = 24 nodes arranged as branches of depth ≤ 4 — exact `BRANCHES`/`REFINE` frozen at Lock 1). A trajectory is the unit of "a tried direction pursued over time"; its score trajectory (best-so-far curve vs cumulative cost) is stored, not just its endpoint.
    43	- **Discovery tree:** all branches grown in one round under one policy on one task panel. On round completion the tree is frozen (content-hashed) and appended to the **trace pool** as a **replay world** (`trace_pool/<round>/` + `manifest.json`, mirroring `trace_pool.py`): frozen worlds are never mutated; the pool only grows.
    44	- **Direction:** a semantically coherent research line spanning many nodes/branches (e.g. "lipid-metabolism genes under medium change, genotype-conditioned"). Directions are the unit the system reasons about when choosing what to try next.
    45	
    46	**0.4.2 The direction catalog (the memory of what was tried).** Maintained at `directions/catalog.json` (versioned, append-only snapshots `directions/history/iterNNN.json`), adapted from Dream-RSI's direction schema. Each direction record carries: canonical name + description; **status** ∈ {`unexplored`, `underexplored`, `promising`, `promising_but_fragile`, `promising_but_underexplored`, `well_explored`, `saturated`, `failed_but_recoverable`, `unpromising`}; linked node/branch IDs (evidence attempts); best/median evaluator quality; quality-vs-cost slope; failure taxonomy counts; heterogeneity/robustness notes; `avoid_repeating` list (exact dead ends: invalid transforms, leaked features, refuted sub-claims); open bottlenecks. Status transitions are rule-based from recorded outcomes (thresholds frozen at Lock 1), not vibes: e.g. `saturated` requires ≥ K refinements with best-so-far improvement < ε over the last k attempts; `promising_but_fragile` requires top-quartile quality that collapses under a prespecified sensitivity check. An extraction step (sol-xhigh, schema-validated JSON, mirroring `directions/extract.py` + `validate.py`) maps each new attempt into the catalog; invalid extractions are rejected and ledgered, never silently coerced.
    47	
    48	**0.4.3 Seeing which directions work best (trajectory scoring & the frontier).** After every round the runner recomputes, deterministically from the ledger (no LLM in this path):
    49	- per-trajectory **best-valid-quality**, **cost-to-threshold**, **area-over-the-curve** (quality vs cumulative measured cost), and **validity rate**;
    50	- per-direction aggregates (above) + a **Pareto frontier** over (scientific quality, total cost, robustness) — directions/trajectories on the frontier are the keepers; dominated ones are candidates for pruning;
    51	- a **direction summary** (sol-xhigh, mirroring Dream-RSI's `GUIDANCE` ablation): compact natural-language digest of explored directions, what worked, what failed and why, and what is conspicuously untried. This summary is injected into the next round's generation prompts so new generations are explicitly conditioned on everything already tried. Summaries are artifacts (hashed, versioned); they guide generation but never alter scores.
    52	
    53	**0.4.4 Keeping, recombining, and generating the next generation (sol-xhigh as the proposer).** Each new generation of candidates is produced by `gpt-6.1-sol` at `xhigh` from a fixed proposer prompt whose inputs are: the direction catalog, the direction summary, the Pareto frontier, remaining budget, and the locked hypothesis/transformation spaces. Every candidate must declare one **origin** (adapted from Dream-RSI's candidate origins) with machine-checked lineage:
    54	- `new` — opens an `unexplored` direction inside the locked spaces;
    55	- `underexplored` — extends a direction whose evidence is thin relative to its promise;
    56	- `combination` (**recombination**) — merges ≥ 2 named parent trajectories/directions (e.g. Parent A's feature family + Parent B's conditioning structure), recording **both parent node IDs** — the child is a DAG node with multiple parents, each parent's contribution stated; recombination may only combine elements that are each legal under Lock 1, and the combined artifact is re-validated from scratch (parents' scores never transfer);
    57	- `repair` — revives a `failed_but_recoverable` direction by fixing its recorded failure mode (e.g. implementation bug, support violation) while preserving its scientific idea; the failure being repaired must be cited by node ID.
    58	Candidates also carry: priority score, target bottleneck, related direction IDs, `avoid_repeating` acknowledgements, and evidence-attempt citations. The runner **rejects** candidates that (a) fall outside locked spaces, (b) duplicate an existing node context-hash, (c) cite nonexistent parents/evidence, or (d) repeat an `avoid_repeating` dead end without a `repair` justification. Rejections are ledgered with reasons — the proposer sees them next round.
    59	
    60	**0.4.5 Selection: who decides what runs next (policy vs proposer — kept distinct).** Two different decisions must never be conflated:
    61	- **Proposal** (sol-xhigh): *what artifacts/directions could be tried* (0.4.4) and *what policy rewrites could be tried* (challengers, Phase 4).
    62	- **Selection** (the executable **policy** + frozen rules): *which legal action executes next* given observed history, legal actions, and remaining budget. The incumbent policy consumes the catalog/frontier (e.g. "refine frontier branches with steepest recent slope until marginal gain < ε, then open the highest-priority underexplored direction; reserve x% for `new`") — but the choice is made by versioned code, replayable and auditable, not by an unrecorded LLM judgment. LLM priority scores are **inputs** to the policy, never the decision itself. This preserves the spec's requirement that improvement mean *changed executed decisions by an executable policy*, while still using sol-xhigh to pick the most promising candidate trajectories to put before the policy.
    63	- **Keep/prune rule (frozen at Lock 1):** frontier trajectories are kept refinable; `saturated`/`unpromising` directions are pruned from the legal-action set (but never deleted from history); `promising_but_fragile` trajectories may only be refined via prespecified robustness diagnostics. Pruning decisions are ledgered so replay can reproduce exactly which actions were legal when.
    64	
    65	**0.4.6 Dreaming: offline policy improvement in replay worlds.** Policy challengers (sol-xhigh rewrites of the policy module, `M = incumbent + ≥3 rewrites`, mirroring the paper's M=4) are evaluated by **replaying them over every frozen world in the trace pool**, once per **budget preference** ρ ∈ {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1} (ρ interpolates quality-vs-cost emphasis in the replay objective, paper Eq. 1 adapted to the locked endpoints). Hard constraints from the specification override the paper where they conflict: replay reveals **only recorded outcomes along the trajectory**; a recorded child replays only on **exact context-hash match** (immutable parent + fixed context), else the action is `unsupported` and scored as unsupported — never imputed; policies are compared on **common support** with coverage reported; replay is a **screen**, and promotion additionally requires fresh online rollouts (Phase 4). Both the learned-policy arm and the **Recursive Fixed Exploration** baseline start from the **same shared first round E0** (explored with the hand-designed starting policy, copied read-only into each arm), so the comparison isolates the value of policy learning itself.
    66	
    67	**0.4.7 Lineage & audit views.** The runner can emit at any time (`status --trajectories`): the full lineage DAG (every node's parents incl. recombination parents), per-direction status board, frontier plot data (quality vs cost per trajectory, colored by generation), generation-over-generation table (best quality, cost-to-threshold, validity rate, support coverage, incumbent ID), and the "graveyard" (pruned directions + why). These views are derived deterministically from the append-only ledger; if a view and the ledger disagree, the ledger wins and the view is a bug.
    68	
    69	### 0.5 Generation protocol — the per-generation operating loop (modeled on the proven `~/bufferright/business` LOOP)
    70	
    71	The business workspace ran 48 generations of an RSI-style loop (`data/research/paths/LOOP/`: `gen1…gen48/`, `LOOP-LEDGER.md`, `engine_loop_v1…v6.py`, `battery.py`) under the rule *"Sol xhigh proposes, Sol high reviews each generation"*, with review score trails like 54→76→92 and explicit family closures. This project adopts that battle-tested operating discipline, translated to the cancer domain (business term → here): **sleeve pool → direction/analysis-family pool; blend → recombination; screen → cheap screen + replay screen; battery → full evaluator battery; PREREG hash → generation-spec hash; forward ledger → frozen predictions; anchor → baseline anchor**.
    72	
    73	**0.5.1 Anatomy of one generation (`generations/genNNN/`, never reused, never overwritten).**
    74	1. **`SPEC.md` + hash, locked before any run.** States: the question this generation discriminates, candidate families to enumerate, screen metric, battery definition, pass bar, stop rules, and trial budget. SHA-256 recorded in the ledger *before* execution (business precedent: `PREREG-*.md` hashes locked pre-download). Amendments are new versioned files (`SPEC-amendment-1.md` + hash + reason), never edits — and any amendment touching locked science follows the new-study-ID rule.
    75	2. **Enumerate from the pool.** Candidates are systematic combinations/mutations of pool elements (feature families × conditioning structures × estimators), each with declared origin + parents per §0.4.4. Sol-xhigh proposes the enumeration and any genuinely new mechanisms; the runner validates legality (locked spaces, dedup by context hash, parent existence).
    76	3. **Screen (cheap, ranking only).** One fast evaluator pass (development panels and/or replay) ranks candidates; screen metrics are approximations and are **labeled as such** — screen-positives are never claims (business precedent: curve metrics marked approximate; pass/fail only at battery).
    77	4. **Recombine winners.** Screen survivors are recombined (`combination` origin, both parents recorded) and mutated around winners (business precedent: "recombine winners", Gen2 weight mutations around G01). Recombinations re-enter at screen; no score inheritance.
    78	5. **Battery (finalists only, decisive).** Full frozen evaluator + validity diagnostics + matched controls + prespecified ablations on fresh development rollouts. *Every* screen-positive gets batteried before any "no candidate passes" verdict (business precedent: M1 remediations completing batteries for all screen-positives).
    79	6. **Verdict + family bookkeeping.** Each family gets an explicit disposition: `ADVANCES`, `DEAD` (with numbers), `CLOSED` (family exhausted; reopening requires new data or a named repair), `INVALID` (defective run — **preserved as a defective record**, never deleted; business precedent: Gen46 v1 look-ahead preserved, corrected in v2/v3), or `BLOCKED-DATA` (needed data unavailable; no proxy substitution without downgrading the claim).
    80	7. **Sol-high review of the generation** (score 0–100, severity-tagged issues) → verify → fix → re-review until **≥ 90**; remediation entries appended per issue (`CONFIRMED+FIXED` / `ACCEPTED-as-implemented with claim downgrade` / `REJECTED with evidence`), exactly as the business ledger records C1/H1/M1 dispositions. The generation is not "complete" below 90.
    81	8. **Ledger the generation** in `GENERATIONS.md` (human-readable, append-only; machine truth remains the JSONL ledgers): candidates screened/batteried, numbers for every finalist, failures with numbers, lessons (e.g. business precedent: "always verify tail direction before screening" → here: "always verify score direction + pairing before screening"), cumulative **trial accounting** (total configurations ever screened — correlated configs are not independent trials, and the count is reported, never hidden), next generation's mandate, and explicit **stop-rule checks** (e.g. leakage found ⇒ dependent mutations cancelled, business precedent: Gen1 look-through cancelling Gen2 weight mutations).
    82	
    83	**0.5.2 Cross-generation invariants borrowed from the business loop.**
    84	- **Anchor regression:** a frozen reference result (here: the development baseline reproduction) must reproduce **byte-identically / within tolerance** after every engine/evaluator change, or the change does not land (business: C06 anchor `0.02506618343648448` identical across engine versions). Engine/evaluator versions are preserved side-by-side (`evaluator_v1.py, v2.py…`), never overwritten.
    85	- **Provenance-stamped runs:** every run file stamps SHA-256 of engine, spec, data, splits, evaluator, and each input artifact; caches invalidate on any mismatch; unstamped rows are exploratory-only (business M6 remediation).

    86	- **Frozen forward evidence:** once a candidate is frozen (Lock 2 predictions), it is judged **only** on untouched outcomes; post-hoc discoveries (e.g. a control arm outperforming its hypothesis — business Gen8/Gen9 controls) are flagged post-hoc, may be frozen for forward judging, and are **never promotable on consumed data**.
    87	- **Negative-EV stop judgments are first-class outcomes:** sol-xhigh + lead investigator may jointly declare further search on consumed panels negative-EV (business Gen5/Gen6 precedent) — recorded as a research judgment with reasons, triggering pivot to new-data directions or honest terminal states rather than endless tuning.
    88	- **One fixed specification for falsification probes:** cheap decisive diagnostics run exactly as specified with no variants (business Gen6 seasonality precedent) — a failed cheapest-falsification closes the family without a variant hunt.
    89	- **Matched controls before mechanism claims:** a new ingredient is credited only against a same-construction control lacking it (business: matched CBOP controls, coverage-matched controls); construction changes without matched controls cannot be decomposed and must be disclosed as such.
    90	
    91	**0.5.3 How sol picks the next trajectories (end-to-end, one generation boundary).**
    92	`ledger + catalog + frontier` → (deterministic) trajectory scores & status board → sol-xhigh writes the **direction summary** and the **ranked next-generation proposal**: which frontier trajectories to keep refining, which two+ parents to recombine (with the scientific rationale for why the combination could work where each parent stalled), which `failed_but_recoverable` lines to repair, which `unexplored` directions to open, and what to prune — each item citing node/direction IDs and the recorded evidence → incumbent **policy code** converts the proposal + budget into the executed action sequence (§0.4.5) → runs execute → extraction updates the catalog → sol-high reviews the generation to ≥ 90 → `GENERATIONS.md` entry + trace-pool append. The loop therefore always generates from the full tried history: nothing is proposed without lineage, nothing survives without battery numbers, and nothing is forgotten — failures remain searchable evidence for every future generation.

   109	
   110	**Do (verify, never assume — every seed is a claim until checked against the primary source):**
   111	1. **Read methods + data availability + supplements** for S1 (Broad NextGen, PMC13581591 / doi:10.1038/s41586-026-10843-7), S2 (Sanger organoids, PMC13581617 / doi:10.1038/s41586-026-10830-y), S3 (Oct 2026 transferability study, PMC13664187 / doi:10.3390/biology15191782 + Zenodo 22472622 code), S5 (Dream-RSI, arXiv:2609.14858 + google-research/dream-rsi). Extract: exact environmental design (screens × models × contrasts), gene-selection rule, score definitions/directions, QC flags, reported lipid/adhesion/state findings (= controls, not discoveries).
   112	2. **Resolve current data routes via official manifests only** (DepMap 26Q3 data page + forum guidance; figshare 29472362 / 28339340; Cell Model Passports; Sanger DataMiner docs). For each file actually retrieved: source URL, release/commit, retrieval date, license, bytes, SHA-256 → `data/source_manifest.csv`. **Never invent a download URL/file ID.** If a portal blocks unattended retrieval, use official alternatives; never bypass a challenge. Prefer **study-specific releases** over the integrated 26Q3 release for development (audit whether 26Q3 corrections used external outcomes; if so, rebuild development-only or treat claims as transductive with limited wording).
   113	3. **Build the provenance table + crosswalk:** biological model, donor, lineage, sample origin, lab, library, medium, format, replicate, release, outcome — across Broad/HCMI/Sanger/author IDs (`data/model_crosswalk.csv`).    114	4. **Assay audit per source:** Chronos vs Sanger fitness — direction, dynamic range, controls, missingness, QC. Confirm paired row-level outcomes + replicates + metadata actually exist for the intended contrasts; if a required pairing is not released, record it as a blocker **before** modeling. Check lab↔biology confounding explicitly (a batch correction cannot separate confounded effects).
   115	5. **Novelty audit v1 (claim ledger):** dated literature search with preserved queries/exclusions (`reviews/novelty_audit.md` + `ledger/claims.jsonl`). For each candidate claim: nearest prior result, what's established, precise remaining uncertainty, evidence needed, consequence if supported/refuted. Explicitly rule out: generic 2D→organoid transfer, published environmental/biomarker findings.
   116	
   117	**Gate G1 (identifiability):** a **named, falsifiable question in a named subset** with counted independent units and a documented paired contrast (or a documented narrower question within the same objective if the original is unsupported — broader goal then recorded unmet for that component).    121	## Phase 2 — Smallest valid vertical slice + baselines + Lock 1 (Gate G2 → C4–C5)
   122	
   123	**Do:**
   124	1. **Estimators (development data only):** paired within-model environmental contrasts in original units (report both conditions, **all individual paired changes**, uncertainty, heterogeneity — a near-zero mean hiding opposing effects must be visible); transfer models with preprocessing/feature selection/tuning **inside development folds only**; uncertainty clustered at donor/model level with effective sample sizes (few clusters ⇒ justify or downgrade the interval/test).
   125	2. **Baselines (same eligible rows, same permitted info):** lineage-only; genotype-only; state/expression-only; genotype+state; +context/assay covariates; regularized linear predictor modeled on the S3 transfer study (reproduce from its Zenodo code where possible). Compare against the **strongest** baseline; prespecify the environmental-information **ablation**. Reproduction needing final outcomes is deferred until after Lock 2.
   126	3. **Evaluator (fixed, separate module, narrow interface):** scores artifacts by effect sizes + calibrated predictive/uncertainty endpoints on development panels. **Never** rewards p-value size, discovery count, or narrative confidence. Includes validity diagnostics + invalid-result rules (leakage, direction flips, support violations ⇒ `invalid`, not zero).
   127	4. **Lock 1 — freeze the research contract** (`PROTOCOL.md` + `contract.json`, SHA-256 hashed with evaluator code + split manifests): exact question + falsifiable alternatives; population/environments/targets/generalization; eligibility; data/ID versions; **A/B/C partitions** (development / reserved policy / final external — donor/model-blocked, overlap recorded); feature & transformation rules; primary/secondary endpoints with fixed direction; practical-effect / equivalence margins with justification (no defensible margin ⇒ no "invariant" label, continuous effects only); multiplicity (default: Holm, familywise α=0.05, small confirmatory family) + uncertainty methods; baselines; invalid rules; success/failure/inconclusive/budget-exhaustion conditions; policy endpoint choice (§7.3). Changing a locked contract ⇒ **new study ID** + new evaluation evidence.
   128	5. **Tests:** identity leakage (donor overlap across A/B/C fails the build), score direction, grouping integrity, estimator behavior on synthetic known-effects (code/calibration only), cost accounting, resume idempotence, unsupported-replay handling, final-outcome access guard (C-layer paths unreadable by proposer code paths; access attempts logged).
   129	
   130	**Gate G2:** baselines reproduced on development data closely enough to establish direction/cohort/measurement integrity (discrepancies resolved or bounded in writing); contract + evaluator + splits hashed and committed. Sol-high review ≥ 90 on contract + evaluator + tests. **No policy optimization before G2.**

   134	## Phase 3 — Pilot: fixed policy → first replay world (Gate G3 → C13 started)
   135	
   136	**Do:**
   137	1. Define the **initial fixed exploration policy** as an executable module: `policy(history, legal_actions, remaining_budget) → action`. Legal actions: open a predefined analysis family, choose a branch, refine an artifact, run a permitted diagnostic, stop. Policy can alter branching/prioritization/explore-vs-refine/stopping — **never** labels, splits, metrics, margins, corrections, success criteria.
   138	2. Run it on real development tasks to the pilot target: **24 evaluated nodes** (generator = sol-xhigh for candidate generation; evaluator fixed; every node ledgered with full hashes + costs + failures). Environmental sensitivity estimates carry uncertainty downstream via a justified measurement-error/resampling/sensitivity treatment — noisy estimates are never treated as exact.
   139	3. Freeze the resulting discovery trees as **Replay World v1** (immutable snapshot + manifest). Replay engine rules: reveal only recorded outcomes along the trajectory; refinement requires immutable parent + **exact context-hash match** (else `unsupported`); never invent counterfactual outcomes; compare policies on **common support** and report support coverage.
   140

   145	## Phase 4 — Policy-improvement cycles (Gate G4 → C8)
   146	
   147	Each cycle below is executed as one or more **generations** under the §0.5 protocol (SPEC-hash → enumerate → screen → recombine → battery → verdict → sol-high ≥ 90 → `GENERATIONS.md` entry). Repeat per cycle (≥ 2 genuine cycles; promotions not required — retained incumbent is a valid outcome):
   148	1. **Generate ≥ 3 executable challengers** with sol-xhigh (+ unchanged incumbent = 4-way comparison). Challengers are code, not narratives; a reflection that changes no executed decision is not a challenger.
   149	2. **Replay** all four under identical resource constraints + support rules on the current replay world (development histories only; final Sanger outcomes never enter the pool).
   150	3. **Select** by the frozen selection rule; **fresh-rollout test** the selected challenger vs fixed exploration (same generator, same evaluator, same ceilings). Charge rewriting + replay + failures + promotion trials to the improving system; baselines may spend the equivalent on search.
   151	4. **Promote only** if the preregistered criterion is met; otherwise **retain incumbent** and keep failed challengers in the ledger. Append only development histories; rebuild replay world version.
   152	5. If replay support is inadequate: preserve that finding, judge by fresh rollouts, and label the method **"Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation"** with departures disclosed.
   153	
   154	**Gate G4:** ≥ 2 cycles fully ledgered (challengers, replay results incl. unsupported rates, rollout comparisons, promotion/retention decisions with reasons). Sol-high review ≥ 90 per cycle record.

   194	## 8. Artifact & command inventory (acceptance checklist)
   195	
   196	**Files:** `README.md` · `GOAL.md` · `PROTOCOL.md` + `contract.json` (hashed) · `STATUS.md` + `status.json` · `GENERATIONS.md` (append-only generation ledger, §0.5) · `generations/genNNN/{SPEC.md,screen/,battery/,sol-review-*.txt}` · `directions/{catalog.json,history/iterNNN.json,extraction/*.jsonl,summaries/}` · `trace_pool/<round>/` + `manifest.json` (frozen replay worlds) · `data/{source_manifest,model_crosswalk,eligibility_registry,split_manifest_*}` · `ledger/{claims,experiments,resources,reviews}` · `src/{adapters,baselines,evaluator,policies,replay,runner,budget}` (evaluator/engine versions preserved side-by-side) · immutable `artifacts/` + checksums · frozen predictions/models/policies · `atlas/evidence_atlas.*` · figures · `manuscript/` + limitations + adversarial-review report · env lock + licenses/attribution · `reproducibility_report.md` · `tests/` (leakage, direction, grouping, estimator, cost, resume, replay-support, final-access, anchor-regression, lineage/DAG integrity, catalog-schema validation).
   197	
   198	**Commands (each documented in README, each ledgering its run):**
   199	`bootstrap` → `audit-data` → `reproduce-baselines` → `run-pilot` → `run-cycle` → `evaluate-policy` → `lock-science` → `evaluate-external` → `reproduce` · `status` · `resume`. Commands refuse out-of-order execution by gate (e.g., `run-cycle` before G2, `evaluate-external` before Lock 2) with the missing gate named.
   200	
   201	## 9. Reporting cadence
   202	
   203	- **First substantive report (at G3):** verified data (manifest + counts), identifiable first question, baseline results, real experiments completed (node count + outcomes), replay support (coverage + unsupported rate), policy retained/promoted, measured resource use (incl. `unknown` costs), next discriminating experiment, exact resume command. If a gate blocks the first cycle: the precise blocker + all completed independent work. Never claim the loop "started" on scaffolding alone.
   204	- **Per-phase:** measured results, unresolved risks, next discriminating experiment. No progress theater (file counts, unexecuted plans).
   205	- **Final:** terminal state, C1–C13 evidence table, three separate status fields, full costs + failures.

   207	## 10. Risks & precommitted responses
   208	
   209	| Risk | Precommitted response |
   210	|---|---|
   211	| Paired environmental outcomes not actually released at row level | Stop at G1; document narrower identifiable question; do not model the missing contrast |
   212	| 7 models ⇒ underpowered / non-identifiable environmental claim | Report continuous effects + heterogeneity; claim only refutations/bounds the precision supports; seek additional public controlled-environment data or keep gate unmet |
   213	| Donor overlap Broad↔Sanger destroys "independent" cohort | Crosswalk-driven filtering; if independence unattainable, downgrade wording + claim scope; never assume independence |
   214	| Integrated-release leakage (outcome-guided corrections) | Study-specific releases + development-fitted transforms; else transductive disclosure with limited claims |
   215	| Assay mismatch (Chronos vs Sanger scores) | Within-study contrasts in original units; cross-study via justified rank/calibration only |
   216	| Replay support too thin to screen challengers | Fresh rollouts decide; label Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation; disclose departures |

   220	| Budget/time exhausted mid-campaign | Checkpoint, `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` + unmet gates + smallest concrete next step; never auto-raise ceilings |
